Oh, um... I definitely don't feel like I'm out of control or anything like that, I promise. I'm sure I could stop if I really wanted to.

Oh, um... distress? I wouldn't use a strong word like that. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over it or letting it ruin my life or anything. I'm generally a very positive person, I promise!

Oh, goodness, no! I definitely haven't stopped going out or become a hermit or anything like that. I'm still very social and I always get all my work done, I promise. I would never let the attorneys down! 

But, um... I guess if I'm being completely honest... sometimes the extra time I spend getting ready in the mornings has made me have to rush into the courthouse a little bit. And... well, I might have missed the very start of my yoga class more than once because I was just trying to get my makeup right. 

And, like I mentioned earlier about the pictures... I have stepped out of a few casual photos at my book club and some community events lately. It’s just easier to volunteer to take the picture instead of being in it, so I don't have to worry about how my face is going to look. 

I still go to everything, though! I just... maybe alter how I participate a tiny bit? I really hope that doesn't make me sound irresponsible or flaky. I just want to look nice. Do you think that's fairly typical for someone just trying to put their best foot forward?

Oh, um... well, I mean, I think it is? Or, at least, I definitely see it. I really wouldn't be here taking up your time if I didn't think it was there, I promise. I'm not trying to be dramatic or make things up. 

But... well, I guess the reason I'm here is because ever since we put in that new, brighter mirror in our bathroom at home, I just keep noticing it. It looks like the skin around my cheeks and my jaw is... uneven? Or maybe a little saggy? 

But the thing is, my husband says he really can't see what I'm talking about. He says I look the same as always. So... I don't know. I mean, I trust him, of course! But the mirror is so bright, and I just see it so clearly. Do you think it's possible that I'm just noticing things that other people might not see because I'm the one looking so closely? I just want to make sure I'm not being completely unreasonable about this, you know?

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's difficult! I really try not to let it take over my day or anything like that. Once I get to the office, I'm completely focused on my work. I would never let my attorneys catch me distracted by something so silly, I promise! 

I can definitely push it aside when I need to focus. I mean... maybe it pops back into my head if I go to the restroom and see the mirror, but I can usually just shake it off and get right back to my desk. I don't just sit around dwelling on it when I have things to do. 

So, it's really not too hard to move past it. That means it's not too serious, right? I really try my best to keep a good attitude and not let it consume my thoughts.

I just wanted to talk to someone about it, if that's alright.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been bothered by the appearance of your skin since noticing it with the new mirror?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I'm actually not entirely sure. We put the mirror up a little while ago, but I didn't want to make a big fuss about it, so I guess I haven't really kept track of exactly how many weeks it's been. \n\nIt hasn't been a super long time, I promise. I really try not to dwell on it too much, you know? I'm sorry I don't have an exact date for you... I hope that's still an okay answer. Is that alright?\n\nDoctor: Has this concern about your skin affected your daily activities or routines in any way?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not in any major way! I mean, I still get all my work done, I promise. I would never let something like this actually get in the way of my job. \n\nBut... um, I guess I have noticed it takes me just a little bit longer to get ready in the mornings now. Just trying to make sure everything looks presentable, you know? Because of that extra time, I... well, I might have had to rush into the courthouse a few mornings lately. And I think I've missed the very start of my yoga class more than once. It's nothing too terrible, though! \n\nAnd, um... I guess I have been avoiding the camera a little bit? Like, at my book club or community events, I’ve skipped being in a few casual photos just because... well, I didn’t want to worry about how my face would look in them. I really hope that doesn't make me sound incredibly vain or self-obsessed. I'm still doing everything I need to do, mostly. Does that make sense? I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking your reflection throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or anything like that. I really try not to be one of those people who is always staring at themselves, you know? \n\nBut... I guess in the mornings, before I head to the courthouse, I do tend to look at my face from a few different angles in the mirror. Just to make sure it doesn't look too noticeable, and maybe I put on just a little bit of extra makeup to try and even things out. It's just a quick check, really! \n\nAnd, um... I guess sometimes when I'm at work, I might catch myself looking at some of the other women there, just sort of... comparing my jawline to theirs? Just to see if mine really is as saggy as I think it is. And like I mentioned, I might skip out on a picture here and there if I'm not feeling great about how I look that day. \n\nI really hope that doesn't sound too strange or like I'm totally fixated on it. I just want to look presentable, you know? Is that... is that normal for someone my age to do?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these extra checks in the mirror or comparisons with others, or can you stop yourself if you want to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I definitely don't feel like I'm out of control or anything like that, I promise. I'm sure I could stop if I really wanted to. \n\nI just... well, I guess I haven't really tried to stop? It's really just a little habit I've picked up lately to make sure my face looks okay. \n\nI could definitely cut back on checking if you think I should! I just haven't really thought about trying to stop before. I'm sorry I don't have a more definitive answer for you... I hope it's okay that I'm not entirely sure. Does that sound alright?\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do you feel when you think about the appearance of your skin?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distress? I wouldn't use a strong word like that. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over it or letting it ruin my life or anything. I'm generally a very positive person, I promise!\n\nI mean, I guess it bothers me a little bit? Like... maybe just a twinge of frustration sometimes, especially when the lighting in the bathroom at the firm is really harsh. It makes me feel just a tiny bit self-conscious, but it's really nothing I can't handle. It's just a normal amount of worry about getting older, right? \n\nI really don't feel overwhelmed by it. It's just... a mild annoyance, maybe? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. I just don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social interactions or events because of concerns about your skin appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! I definitely haven't stopped going out or become a hermit or anything like that. I'm still very social and I always get all my work done, I promise. I would never let the attorneys down! \n\nBut, um... I have stepped out of a few casual photos at my book club and some community events lately. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there.     "role": "user",

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, um... well, I wouldn't call it a habit, really! I try to be very intentional about my morning routine, you know? I want to look professional for the attorneys and the clients. 

So, most mornings, I just... I might check my face from a few different angles in that mirror, just to make sure everything looks okay. And maybe I'll put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse, just to smooth things out. It's just normal grooming, right? I want to put my best foot forward.

And... well, sometimes at work, I might notice the other women, the attorneys or the other paralegals, and just sort of... compare my face to theirs? Just a little bit! Not in a mean way, just noticing. And if they're taking casual pictures around the office and I'm not feeling my best that day, I might just step out of the frame. I don't want to ruin their photos if I'm not looking perfectly put together. 

That's all pretty normal for a woman my age, don't you think? I'm just trying to be careful about how I present myself. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for!

Just to make sure it doesn't look too noticeable, and maybe I put on just a little bit of extra makeup to try and even things out. It's just a quick check, really! \n\nAnd, um... I guess sometimes when I'm at work, I might catch myself looking at some of the other women there, just sort of... comparing my jawline to theirs? Just to see if mine really is as saggy as I think it is. And like I mentioned, I might skip out on a picture here and there if I'm not feeling great about how I look that day. \n\nI really hope that doesn't sound too strange or like I'm totally fixated on it. I just want to look presentable, you know? Is that... is that normal for someone my age to do?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these extra checks in the mirror or comparisons with others, or can you stop yourself if you want to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I definitely don't feel like I'm out of control or anything like that, I promise. I'm sure I could stop if I really wanted to. \n\nI just... well, I guess I haven't really tried to stop? Because to me, it just feels like I'm being careful, you know? Just making sure I look professional for the attorneys at the firm. So I wouldn't say I *have* to do it, like some kind of compulsion. It's really just a little habit I've picked up lately to make sure my face looks okay. \n\nI could definitely cut back on checking if you think I should! I just haven't really thought about trying to stop before. I'm sorry I don't have a more definitive answer for you... I hope it's okay that I'm not entirely sure. Does that sound alright?\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do you feel when you think about the appearance of your skin?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distress? I wouldn't use a strong word like that. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over it or letting it ruin my life or anything. I'm generally a very positive person, I promise!\n\nI mean, I guess it bothers me a little bit? Like... maybe just a twinge of frustration sometimes, especially when the lighting in the bathroom at the firm is really harsh. It makes me feel just a tiny bit self-conscious, but it's really nothing I can't handle. It's just a normal amount of worry about getting older, right? \n\nI really don't feel overwhelmed by it. It's just... a mild annoyance, maybe? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. I just don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social interactions or events because of concerns about your skin appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! I definitely haven't stopped going out or become a hermit or anything like that. I'm still very social and I always get all my work done, I promise. I would never let the attorneys down! \n\nBut, um... I guess if I'm being completely honest... sometimes the extra time I spend getting ready in the mornings has made me have to rush into the courthouse a little bit. And... well, I might have missed the very start of my yoga class more than once because I was just trying to get my makeup right. \n\nAnd, like I mentioned earlier about the pictures... I have stepped out of a few casual photos at my book club and some community events lately. It’s just easier to volunteer to take the picture instead of being in it, so I don't have to worry about how my face is going to look. \n\nI still go to everything, though! I just... maybe alter how I participate a tiny bit? I really hope that doesn't make me sound irresponsible or flaky. I just want to look nice. Do you think that's fairly typical for someone just trying to put their best foot forward?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any impact on your relationships with family or friends due to your concerns about your skin?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not! My relationships are wonderful, really. I have a great group of friends, and my family is so supportive. I wouldn't let something like this get in the way of the people I care about. I promise I'm still very present with everyone.\n\nI mean... I guess my husband has mentioned a couple of times that I've been spending a little longer in the bathroom lately? But he's so sweet about it, he just teases me a little. And maybe my friends have noticed that I'm the one always volunteering to hold the camera now, but they haven't said anything negative, I swear. \n\nIt really hasn't caused any arguments or tension or anything bad like that. I always make sure to be a good friend and a good wife. I just... I like to look put-together for them, you know? That's totally normal, isn't it? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm letting this affect my personal life too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your skin's unevenness or sagging is an accurate reflection of how you actually look?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean, I think it is? Or, at least, I definitely see it. I really wouldn't be here taking up your time if I didn't think it was there, I promise. I'm not trying to be dramatic or make things up. \n\nBut... well, I guess the reason I'm here is because ever since we put in that new, brighter mirror in our bathroom at home, I just keep noticing it. It looks like the skin around my cheeks and my jaw is... uneven? Or maybe a little saggy? \n\nBut the thing is, my husband says he really can't see what I'm talking about. He says I look the same as always. So... I don't know. I mean, I trust him, of course! But the mirror is so bright, and I just see it so clearly. Do you think it's possible that I'm just noticing things that other people might not see because I'm the one looking so closely? I just want to make sure I'm not being completely unreasonable about this, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to push the thoughts about your skin's appearance out of your mind once they come up?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's difficult! I really try not to let it take over my day or anything like that. Once I get to the office, I'm completely focused on my work. I would never let my attorneys catch me distracted by something so silly, I promise! \n\nI can definitely push it aside when I need to focus. I mean... maybe it pops back into my head if I go to the restroom and see the mirror, but I can usually just shake it off and get right back to my desk. I don't just sit around dwelling on it when I have things to do. \n\nSo, it's really not too hard to move past it. That means it's not too serious, right? I really try my best to keep a good attitude and not let it consume my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin isn't actually there, even if you're convinced it is?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean... I suppose anything is possible? I certainly don't want to seem like I'm completely closed off to the idea. I know you're the expert here, and I'd never want to argue with you! \n\nBut... well, like I mentioned earlier, it really started when we got that new, brighter bathroom mirror. I just... I look in it, and the skin around my cheeks and my jaw just looks so uneven to me. And maybe a little saggy? I see it so clearly when I'm standing there. \n\nBut then, you know, my husband says he honestly can't see what I'm talking about. And he's such a good, honest man, I know he wouldn't just lie to me. So... I guess, if he doesn't see it, maybe it's possible it's not as bad as I think? Or maybe it's just the lighting playing tricks on me? I really hope I'm just overreacting. It would actually be quite a relief if it wasn't really there, you know? Does that make sense? I'm trying to keep an open mind about it, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin is something that only you can see, and not something that would be noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I think that's definitely possible. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm talking in circles! I don't want to waste your time saying the same things over and over. \n\nBut... well, like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here is because ever since we put in that brighter bathroom mirror, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. But since my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, maybe it really is just something only I can see? \n\nI mean, I really hope that's the case! It would just mean I'm being a little too picky about myself, which isn't the worst thing in the world, right? I'm trying to be very reasonable about it, I promise. Am I answering your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking your reflection is something you do intentionally or is it more of an automatic habit?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't call it a habit, really! I try to be very intentional about my morning routine, you know? I want to look professional for the attorneys and the clients. \n\nSo, most mornings, I just... I might check my face from a few different angles in that mirror, just to make sure everything looks okay. And maybe I'll put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse, just to smooth things out. It's just normal grooming, right? I want to put my best foot forward.\n\nAnd... well, sometimes at work, I might notice the other women, the attorneys or the other paralegals, and just sort of... compare my face to theirs? Just a little bit! Not in a mean way, just noticing. And if they're taking casual pictures around the office and I'm not feeling my best that day, I might just step out of the frame. I don't want to ruin their photos if I'm not looking perfectly put together. \n\nThat's all pretty normal for a woman my age, don't you think? I'm just trying to be careful about how I present myself. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for!\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about the appearance of your skin when you're not intentionally trying to think about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, when I'm at work or busy with things, my mind is completely on the attorneys and the case files. I'm not just sitting there thinking about my face when I shouldn't be, I promise! \n\nI try to be very focused and professional. I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm obsessing over it when I'm supposed to be working or taking care of the house. So, no, it doesn't really pop into my head out of nowhere. Is that... is that helpful for you to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin is not noticeable to others because it's not actually there, rather than just because they're not looking closely enough?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose that's possible, yes. I try very hard to be reasonable and not... well, not imagine things that aren't there. \n\nSo, yes, if you think it's possible that it's just not there, I'm more than happy to believe that! I really trust your judgment on this. Does that make sense? Because to me, it just feels like I'm being careful, you know? Just making sure I look professional for the attorneys at the firm. So I wouldn't say I *have* to do it, like some kind of compulsion. It's really just a little habit I've picked up lately to make sure my face looks okay. \n\nI could definitely cut back on checking if you think I should! I just haven't really thought about trying to stop before. I'm sorry I don't have a more definitive answer for you... I hope it's okay that I'm not entirely sure. Does that sound alright?\n\nDoctor: How much distress or anxiety do you feel when you think about the appearance of your skin?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distress? I wouldn't use a strong word like that. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over it or letting it ruin my life or anything. I'm generally a very positive person, I promise!\n\nI mean, I guess it bothers me a little bit? Like... maybe just a twinge of frustration sometimes, especially when the lighting in the bathroom at the firm is really harsh. It makes me feel just a tiny bit self-conscious, but it's really nothing I can't handle. It's just a normal amount of worry about getting older, right? \n\nI really don't feel overwhelmed by it. It's just... a mild annoyance, maybe? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. I just don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social interactions or events because of concerns about your skin appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! I definitely haven't stopped going out or become a hermit or anything like that. I'm still very social and I always get all my work done, I promise. I would never let the attorneys down! \n\nBut, um... I guess if I'm being completely honest... sometimes the extra time I spend getting ready in the mornings has made me have to rush into the courthouse a little bit. I have stepped out of a few casual photos at my book club and some community events lately. It’s just easier to volunteer to take the picture instead of being in it, so I don't have to worry about how my face is going to look. \n\nI still go to everything, though! I just... maybe alter how I participate a tiny bit? I really hope that doesn't make me sound irresponsible or flaky. I just want to look nice. Do you think that's fairly typical for someone just trying to put their best foot forward?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any impact on your relationships with family or friends due to your concerns about your skin?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, definitely not! My relationships are wonderful, really. I have a great group of friends, and my family is so supportive. I wouldn't let something like this get in the way of the people I care about. I promise I'm still very present with everyone.\n\nI mean... I guess my husband has mentioned a couple of times that I've been spending a little longer in the bathroom lately? But he's so sweet about it, he just teases me a little. And maybe my friends have noticed that I'm the one always volunteering to hold the camera now, but they haven't said anything negative, I swear. \n\nIt really hasn't caused any arguments or tension or anything bad like that. I always make sure to be a good friend and a good wife. I just... I like to look put-together for them, you know? That's totally normal, isn't it? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm letting this affect my personal life too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your skin's unevenness or sagging is an accurate reflection of how you actually look?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean, I think it is? Or, at least, I definitely see it. I really wouldn't be here taking up your time if I didn't think it was there, I promise. I'm not trying to be dramatic or make things up. \n\nBut... well, I guess the reason I'm here is because ever since we put in that new, brighter mirror in our bathroom at home, I just keep noticing it. It looks like the skin around my cheeks and my jaw is... uneven? Or maybe a little saggy? \n\nBut the thing is, my husband says he really can't see what I'm talking about. He says I look the same as always. So... I don't know. I mean, I trust him, of course! But the mirror is so bright, and I just see it so clearly. Do you think it's possible that I'm just noticing things that other people might not see because I'm the one looking so closely? I just want to make sure I'm not being completely unreasonable about this, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to push the thoughts about your skin's appearance out of your mind once they come up?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's difficult! I really try not to let it take over my day or anything like that. Once I get to the office, I'm completely focused on my work. I would never let my attorneys catch me distracted by something so silly, I promise! \n\nI can definitely push it aside when I need to focus. I mean... maybe it pops back into my head if I go to the restroom and see the mirror, but I can usually just shake it off and get right back to my desk. I don't just sit around dwelling on it when I have things to do. \n\nSo, it's really not too hard to move past it. That means it's not too serious, right? I really try my best to keep a good attitude and not let it consume my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin isn't actually there, even if you're convinced it is?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean... I see it so clearly when I'm standing there. \n\nBut then, you know, my husband says he honestly can't see what I'm talking about. And he's such a good, honest man, I know he wouldn't just lie to me. I'm trying to keep an open mind about it, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin is something that only you can see, and not something that would be noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I think that's definitely possible. It’s just easier to volunteer to take the picture instead of being in it, so I don't have to worry about how my face is going to look. \n\nI still go to everything, though! I just... maybe alter how I participate a tiny bit? I really hope that doesn't make me sound irresponsible or flaky. I just want to look nice. I promise I'm still very present with everyone.\n\nI mean... I guess my husband has mentioned a couple of times that I've been spending a little longer in the bathroom lately? I always make sure to be a good friend and a good wife. I just... I like to look put-together for them, you know? That's totally normal, isn't it? I hope it doesn't sound like I'm letting this affect my personal life too much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your perception of your skin's unevenness or sagging is an accurate reflection of how you actually look?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean, I think it is? Or, at least, I definitely see it. I really wouldn't be here taking up your time if I didn't think it was there, I promise. I'm not trying to be dramatic or make things up. \n\nBut... well, I guess the reason I'm here is because ever since we put in that new, brighter mirror in our bathroom at home, I just keep noticing it. It looks like the skin around my cheeks and my jaw is... uneven? Or maybe a little saggy? \n\nBut the thing is, my husband says he really can't see what I'm talking about. He says I look the same as always. So... I don't know. I mean, I trust him, of course! But the mirror is so bright, and I just see it so clearly. Do you think it's possible that I'm just noticing things that other people might not see because I'm the one looking so closely? I just want to make sure I'm not being completely unreasonable about this, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to push the thoughts about your skin's appearance out of your mind once they come up?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's difficult! I really try not to let it take over my day or anything like that. Once I get to the office, I'm completely focused on my work. I would never let my attorneys catch me distracted by something so silly, I promise! \n\nI can definitely push it aside when I need to focus. Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say it holds me back, exactly. I mean, I always make sure I get all my work done at the law firm, of course. I'm very responsible about that. 

I suppose... well, sometimes my morning routine takes just a little longer than I planned. Just making sure everything looks presentable, you know? I don't let it stop me from going.

Just because I don't want to be worrying about how my face looks in the lighting, you know? It's just easier that way, and I'm still participating! I just try to handle it quietly so I don't bother anyone. 

That's normal, though, isn't it? A lot of women prefer not to be photographed if they aren't feeling their best. I hope that doesn't sound too silly.

Like, at my book club or community events, I’ve skipped being in a few casual photos just because... well, I didn’t want to worry about how my face would look in them. I hope I'm answering your question right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself repeatedly checking your reflection throughout the day?\n\nPatient: Um, well... I wouldn't say I'm obsessed with it or anything like that. I really try not to be one of those people who is always staring at themselves, you know? \n\nBut... I guess in the mornings, before I head to the courthouse, I do tend to look at my face from a few different angles in the mirror. Just to make sure it doesn't look too noticeable, and maybe I put on just a little bit of extra makeup to try and even things out. It's just a quick check, really! \n\nAnd, um... I guess sometimes when I'm at work, I might catch myself looking at some of the other women there, just sort of... comparing my jawline to theirs? Just to see if mine really is as saggy as I think it is. And like I mentioned, I might skip out on a picture here and there if I'm not feeling great about how I look that day. \n\nI really hope that doesn't sound too strange or like I'm totally fixated on it. I just want to look presentable, you know? Is that... is that normal for someone my age to do?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you have to perform these extra checks in the mirror or comparisons with others, or can you stop yourself if you want to?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... I definitely don't feel like I'm out of control or anything like that, I promise. I'm sure I could stop if I really wanted to. \n\nI just... well, I guess I haven't really tried to stop? Because to me, it just feels like I'm being careful, you know? Just making sure I look professional for the attorneys at the firm. So I wouldn't say I *have* to do it, like some kind of compulsion. It's really just a little habit I've picked up lately to make sure my face looks okay. \n\nI could definitely cut back on checking if you think I should! I just haven't really thought about trying to stop before. I'm sorry I don't have a more definitive answer for you... I hope it's okay that I'm not entirely sure. I really don't want you to think I'm sitting around crying over it or letting it ruin my life or anything. I'm generally a very positive person, I promise!\n\nI mean, I guess it bothers me a little bit? It's just... a mild annoyance, maybe? I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for. I just don't want to make a mountain out of a molehill, you know?\n\nDoctor: Have you avoided any social interactions or events because of concerns about your skin appearance?\n\nPatient: Oh, goodness, no! Am I answering your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking your reflection is something you do intentionally or is it more of an automatic habit?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't call it a habit, really! I try to be very intentional about my morning routine, you know? I want to look professional for the attorneys and the clients. \n\nSo, most mornings, I just... I might check my face from a few different angles in that mirror, just to make sure everything looks okay. And maybe I'll put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse, just to smooth things out. It's just normal grooming, right? I want to put my best foot forward.\n\nAnd... well, sometimes at work, I might notice the other women, the attorneys or the other paralegals, and just sort of... compare my face to theirs? Just a little bit! Not in a mean way, just noticing. And if they're taking casual pictures around the office and I'm not feeling my best that day, I might just step out of the frame. I don't want to ruin their photos if I'm not looking perfectly put together. \n\nThat's all pretty normal for a woman my age, don't you think? I'm just trying to be careful about how I present myself. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for!\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about the appearance of your skin when you're not intentionally trying to think about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't really think so. I mean, when I'm at work or busy with things, my mind is completely on the attorneys and the case files. I'm not just sitting there thinking about my face when I shouldn't be, I promise! \n\nI try to be very focused and professional. Once I'm done with my morning routine and I've made sure everything looks presentable, I just go about my day. I hope that's a good thing? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm obsessing over it when I'm supposed to be working or taking care of the house. So, no, it doesn't really pop into my head out of nowhere. Is that... is that helpful for you to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin is not noticeable to others because it's not actually there, rather than just because they're not looking closely enough?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose that's possible, yes. I mean, my husband says he doesn't see it, so maybe it really isn't there at all? \n\nI'd like to think that's true! It would certainly mean I don't have anything to worry about, right? And I really don't want to be making a big deal out of nothing. I try very hard to be reasonable and not... well, not imagine things that aren't there. \n\nSo, yes, if you think it's possible that it's just not there, I'm more than happy to believe that! I really trust your judgment on this. Does that make sense? I hope I'm understanding your question correctly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your skin when you're trying to fall asleep or relax?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, not really. When I'm trying to relax, I really try to just focus on unwinding, you know? Like reading a book or watching a little television with my husband. \n\nI certainly wouldn't want to ruin a nice evening by worrying about my skin! I try to keep those thoughts for when I'm actually getting ready and doing my routine. Oh, um... thinking about it when I'm not trying to? Well, I try very hard to stay focused on my work. You know, drafting documents and organizing files takes a lot of concentration. 

But... well, I suppose sometimes it does pop into my head. It really started, um, a little while ago when my husband and I put in a new, brighter mirror in the bathroom. Ever since then, I just... I keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks kind of uneven to me, or maybe a little saggy. 

And sometimes, if I'm at my desk or driving, I might catch myself wondering if it looks as noticeable in the daylight as it does in that bathroom mirror. My husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, bless his heart, he's very sweet to say that. But I know it's there. 

I try to just push the thought away and get back to what I'm doing, though. I don't let it consume me or anything. It's perfectly normal to be a little aware of these things as we get older, right? I just want to make sure I'm keeping up appearances. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm obsessing over it.

He says I look the same as always. So... I don't know. I mean, I trust him, of course! But the mirror is so bright, and I just see it so clearly. Do you think it's possible that I'm just noticing things that other people might not see because I'm the one looking so closely? I just want to make sure I'm not being completely unreasonable about this, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you find it difficult to push the thoughts about your skin's appearance out of your mind once they come up?\n\nPatient: Oh, no, I wouldn't say it's difficult! I really try not to let it take over my day or anything like that. Once I get to the office, I'm completely focused on my work. I would never let my attorneys catch me distracted by something so silly, I promise! \n\nI can definitely push it aside when I need to focus. I mean... maybe it pops back into my head if I go to the restroom and see the mirror, but I can usually just shake it off and get right back to my desk. I don't just sit around dwelling on it when I have things to do. \n\nSo, it's really not too hard to move past it. That means it's not too serious, right? I really try my best to keep a good attitude and not let it consume my thoughts.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's a possibility that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin isn't actually there, even if you're convinced it is?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I mean... I suppose anything is possible? I certainly don't want to seem like I'm completely closed off to the idea. I know you're the expert here, and I'd never want to argue with you! \n\nBut... well, like I mentioned earlier, it really started when we got that new, brighter bathroom mirror. I just... I look in it, and the skin around my cheeks and my jaw just looks so uneven to me. And maybe a little saggy? I see it so clearly when I'm standing there. \n\nBut then, you know, my husband says he honestly can't see what I'm talking about. And he's such a good, honest man, I know he wouldn't just lie to me. So... I guess, if he doesn't see it, maybe it's possible it's not as bad as I think? Or maybe it's just the lighting playing tricks on me? I really hope I'm just overreacting. It would actually be quite a relief if it wasn't really there, you know? Does that make sense? I'm trying to keep an open mind about it, I promise.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin is something that only you can see, and not something that would be noticeable to others?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... yes, I think that's definitely possible. I know I keep repeating myself, and I'm so sorry if I'm talking in circles! I don't want to waste your time saying the same things over and over. \n\nBut... well, like I mentioned, the whole reason I'm here is because ever since we put in that brighter bathroom mirror, I just keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks uneven, or kind of saggy to me. But since my husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, maybe it really is just something only I can see? \n\nI mean, I really hope that's the case! It would just mean I'm being a little too picky about myself, which isn't the worst thing in the world, right? I'm trying to be very reasonable about it, I promise. Am I answering your question okay?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like checking your reflection is something you do intentionally or is it more of an automatic habit?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't call it a habit, really! I try to be very intentional about my morning routine, you know? I want to look professional for the attorneys and the clients. \n\nSo, most mornings, I just... I might check my face from a few different angles in that mirror, just to make sure everything looks okay. And maybe I'll put on a little extra makeup before I leave for the courthouse, just to smooth things out. It's just normal grooming, right? I want to put my best foot forward.\n\nAnd... well, sometimes at work, I might notice the other women, the attorneys or the other paralegals, and just sort of... compare my face to theirs? Just a little bit! Not in a mean way, just noticing. And if they're taking casual pictures around the office and I'm not feeling my best that day, I might just step out of the frame. I don't want to ruin their photos if I'm not looking perfectly put together. \n\nThat's all pretty normal for a woman my age, don't you think? I'm just trying to be careful about how I present myself. I hope that's the kind of answer you were looking for!\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about the appearance of your skin when you're not intentionally trying to think about it?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... no, I don't really think so. I'm not just sitting there thinking about my face when I shouldn't be, I promise! \n\nI try to be very focused and professional. Once I'm done with my morning routine and I've made sure everything looks presentable, I just go about my day. I hope that's a good thing? I certainly wouldn't want you to think I'm obsessing over it when I'm supposed to be working or taking care of the house. So, no, it doesn't really pop into my head out of nowhere. Is that... is that helpful for you to know?\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin is not noticeable to others because it's not actually there, rather than just because they're not looking closely enough?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I suppose that's possible, yes. I mean, my husband says he doesn't see it, so maybe it really isn't there at all? \n\nI'd like to think that's true! It would certainly mean I don't have anything to worry about, right? And I really don't want to be making a big deal out of nothing. I try very hard to be reasonable and not... well, not imagine things that aren't there. \n\nSo, yes, if you think it's possible that it's just not there, I'm more than happy to believe that! I hope I'm understanding your question correctly.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your skin when you're trying to fall asleep or relax?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, no, not really. I hope I'm doing that part right.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your concerns about the appearance of your skin are causing you more than a little bit of distress?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... distress? Well, I wouldn't say it's causing me distress, really. I try to be very strong and handle things on my own, you know? I wouldn't want you to think I'm falling apart over a few wrinkles or some unevenness. \n\nIt's just a mild annoyance, really. Something I'm trying to fix with my creams and routines. I try very hard to keep it in perspective. I'm doing okay with that, right? I hope I'm not making it sound worse than it is.\n\nDoctor: Are there any activities or situations where you feel like your concerns about your skin are holding you back or making it difficult for you to participate?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... well, I wouldn't say it holds me back, exactly. I just try to handle it quietly so I don't bother anyone. \n\nThat's normal, though, isn't it? A lot of women prefer not to be photographed if they aren't feeling their best. I hope that doesn't sound too silly.\n\nDoctor: Do you think it's possible that the unevenness or sagging you see in your skin is something that you're imagining and not actually there?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... imagining it? No, I... I don't think I'm imagining it. I mean, I see it right there in the mirror when I'm getting ready. \n\nI know it might not be the most obvious thing in the world to everyone else, and I really do try my best to cover it up and smooth it out so it doesn't look terrible. But it's definitely there. I wouldn't just make something like that up, you know? I try to be very grounded and practical about these things.\n\nI suppose maybe I focus on it a little more than other people might focus on their own skin, just because I want to look professional for the attorneys and clients at the firm. But it's a real thing I'm trying to manage. I hope you don't think I'm just being vain or... or making up problems for myself. I really am just trying to take care of my appearance the best I can. That makes sense, right?\n\nDoctor: Do you ever find yourself thinking about your skin when you're not trying to, like when you're in the middle of doing something else?\n\nPatient: Oh, um... thinking about it when I'm not trying to? Well, I try very hard to stay focused on my work. You know, drafting documents and organizing files takes a lot of concentration. \n\nBut... well, I suppose sometimes it does pop into my head. It really started, um, a little while ago when my husband and I put in a new, brighter mirror in the bathroom. Ever since then, I just... I keep noticing that the skin around my cheeks and jaw looks kind of uneven to me, or maybe a little saggy. \n\nAnd sometimes, if I'm at my desk or driving, I might catch myself wondering if it looks as noticeable in the daylight as it does in that bathroom mirror. My husband says he can't really see what I'm talking about, bless his heart, he's very sweet to say that. But I know it's there. \n\nI try to just push the thought away and get back to what I'm doing, though. I don't let it consume me or anything. It's perfectly normal to be a little aware of these things as we get older, right? I just want to make sure I'm keeping up appearances. I hope that doesn't sound like I'm obsessing over it."
